# Appendix N Resource Ledger of Arithmetic Elimination: Exact Circuits Are Not Sparse Algorithms {#sec-ch34-18-5}

This appendix continues the Schur-complement analysis of finite-memory Gram windows from [Chapter 34, §34.7](ch34-s18-gram-window-and-consensus.md).

Earlier subsections used the Schur complement to characterize single-step margins on finite memory windows; testing elimination ideas on a finite integer divisibility network requires auditing graph fill-in, approximation residuals, and storage costs with the same discipline used in [§22.4.1](ch22-s04-critical-line-and-rh.md#sec-ch22-4-1) to separate series truncation from analytic continuation. Consider the explicit finite vertex set $V_N := \{2, 3, \dots, N\}$: place an undirected edge between $d, n \in V_N$ if and only if $d \neq n$ and ($d \mid n$ or $n \mid d$), write $W$ for the symmetric $0/1$ adjacency matrix and $L_D := \operatorname{diag}(\deg) - W$ for the divisibility-graph Laplacian, and fix the anchor damping $\alpha = \frac{1}{4}$ to define the real symmetric model matrix

$$
G \;:=\; L_D + \alpha I, \qquad \alpha = \frac{1}{4}.
$$

For every nonzero real vector $x = (x_2, \dots, x_N)^\top \neq \mathbf{0}$, completing the square edge by edge yields

$$
x^\top G x \;=\; \alpha\sum_{n=2}^{N} x_n^2 \;+\; \sum_{\substack{2 \le d < n \le N \\ d \mid n}} (x_d - x_n)^2 \;>\; 0.
$$

This elementary sum-of-squares identity guarantees that $G$ is strictly positive definite and invertible, yet three boundaries must be stated upfront: first, $G = L_D + \frac{1}{4}I$ is only a **toy model matrix on a finite divisibility graph and is not equal to the Nyman–Beurling–Báez-Duarte (NBB) Gram matrix** in Hilbert space; second, $G = L_D + \frac{1}{4}I$ is symmetric strictly diagonally dominant (SDD), so subcubic iterative and graph-Laplacian solvers for $G x = b$ already exist in numerical linear algebra (and such known fast solvers for a finite divisibility graph never constitute progress on the Riemann Hypothesis), whereas unpivoted direct Gaussian–Schur elimination still suffers dense fill-in; third, [@thm-ch34-gram-window-schur-and-consensus] covers only abstract inner-product realizations, two- and three-anchor determinant criteria, orthogonal-complement escape, and finite-register dimension bounds—it **cannot be extrapolated to cover the divisibility matrix $G$, Schur elimination complexity, or the Riemann Hypothesis**, and for singular positive-semidefinite Gram blocks (such as $\alpha = 0$, where isolated vertices and component-wise constant vectors lie in the nullspace) the ordinary inverse $A^{-1}$ does not exist and must be replaced by range conditions and the Moore–Penrose pseudoinverse. The interactive laboratory holds fixed the terminal set $T_N = \{n \in V_N : 4n > 3N\}$, and both the natural and leaf-first orders eliminate only its complement $V_N \setminus T_N$ without changing the terminals across orderings. On the $N = 6$ divisibility graph $V_6 = \{2, 3, 4, 5, 6\}$ (with fixed terminals $T_6 = \{5, 6\}$), there are only three original edges $2\text{--}4$, $2\text{--}6$, and $3\text{--}6$, with vertex $5$ **isolated**; eliminating the hub vertex $2$ first immediately creates a nonzero **fill-in edge $4\text{--}6$** of weight $-\frac{4}{9}$ between the previously non-adjacent vertices $4$ and $6$.

::: {.viz src="arithmetic-schur-lab" preset="natural" height="500"}
**Three-dimensional divisibility network with fill-in edges under natural increasing elimination (`natural`).** The 3D stage displays the cyan original divisibility edges and isolated vertices of the integer divisibility network along the purple coordinate axes as interior vertices are eliminated in natural order relative to a fixed terminal set. Eliminating small high-degree hubs first creates magenta fill-in edges between previously unlinked multiples (such as $4\text{--}6$ at $N = 6$), with the amber marker indicating the selected vertex.
:::

For a block matrix $\begin{pmatrix} A & B \\ B^\top & C \end{pmatrix}$, eliminating the interior set $U$ produces the exact one-shot Schur complement $S = C - B^\top A^{-1} B$. **When comparing elimination orderings, the terminal set must never shift with the ordering; only locking a single fixed terminal set $T = V_N \setminus U$ in advance provides a well-defined comparison baseline**.

::: {.example #ex-ch34-divisibility-n6-schur-and-drop title="Verification of Natural vs. Leaf-First Schur Blocks for Fixed Terminals {5, 6} and Drop-Threshold Residual Criterion"}
On $V_6 = \{2, 3, 4, 5, 6\}$ ($\alpha = \frac{1}{4}$), lock the fixed terminal set $T = \{5, 6\}$ and interior elimination set $U = \{2, 3, 4\}$.

1. **Natural order $(2, 3, 4)$ vs. leaf-first order $(4, 3, 2)$**: Eliminating vertex $2$ first in natural order ($G_{22} = \frac{9}{4}$, $G_{24} = G_{26} = -1$) immediately introduces the nonzero fill-in entry $G^{(1)}_{46} = G^{(1)}_{64} = 0 - \frac{(-1)(-1)}{9/4} = -\frac{4}{9}$ across $4\text{--}6$, updating the diagonals to $G^{(1)}_{44} = \frac{29}{36}$ and $G^{(1)}_{66} = \frac{65}{36}$; eliminating $3$ ($G^{(1)}_{33} = \frac{5}{4}$, $G^{(1)}_{36} = -1$) gives $G^{(2)}_{66} = \frac{65}{36} - \frac{4}{5} = \frac{181}{180}$; finally eliminating $4$ yields $S_{66} = \frac{181}{180} - \frac{(4/9)^2}{29/36} = \frac{181}{180} - \frac{64}{261} = \frac{441}{580}$. Keeping the exact same terminal set $T = \{5, 6\}$ under **leaf-first order $(4, 3, 2)$**: eliminating degree-one leaf $4$ (connected only to $2$, $G_{44} = \frac{5}{4}$) updates only $G'_{22} = \frac{9}{4} - \frac{4}{5} = \frac{29}{20}$; eliminating degree-one leaf $3$ (connected only to $6$, $G'_{33} = \frac{5}{4}$) updates only $G''_{66} = \frac{29}{20}$; finally eliminating vertex $2$ (now linked solely to terminal $6$) yields $S_{66} = \frac{29}{20} - \frac{20}{29} = \frac{441}{580}$ with **zero fill-in throughout**, matching the one-shot Schur block $C - B^\top A^{-1} B$ identically:

   $$
   S_{\{5, 6\}} \;=\; \operatorname{diag}\!\left(\frac{1}{4},\; \frac{441}{580}\right) \;=\; \begin{pmatrix} \frac{1}{4} & 0 \\ 0 & \frac{441}{580} \end{pmatrix},
   $$

   where the isolated terminal $5$ retains diagonal entry $\frac{1}{4}$ at every step.
2. **Final Schur-block threshold pruning and original-system residual criteria**: The page first performs exact elimination onto the fixed terminal set $T_N$ to form the final Schur block $S$, then symmetrically zeroes off-diagonal entries satisfying $|S_{ij}| < \varepsilon$ ($i \neq j$) while leaving the diagonal entries untouched to obtain $\tilde{S}$; it solves this approximate terminal system and back-substitutes for $b = e_N$ using the exact elimination record to produce $\tilde{x}$. The laboratory jointly measures the Schur-block discrepancy $\lVert S - \tilde{S}\rVert_F$ and the **original-system residual $\lVert G\tilde{x} - b\rVert_2$**; $\tilde{x}$ can deviate from $G x = b$ only when nonzero off-diagonal terminal couplings fall below $\varepsilon$ and are pruned.
:::

::: {.viz src="arithmetic-schur-lab" preset="leaf" height="500"}
**Three-dimensional divisibility network suppressing fill-in under leaf-first elimination (`leaf`).** The 3D stage shows the cyan original divisibility edges of the integer network along the purple coordinate axes for the same fixed terminal set while degree-one leaves are eliminated ahead of interior hubs. For $N = 6$ with fixed terminals $\{5, 6\}$, the leaf-first sequence $(4, 3, 2)$ generates zero magenta fill-in edges, with the amber marker indicating the selected vertex.
:::

This example underscores a general principle: **the algebraic exactness of the Schur identity $S = C - B^\top A^{-1} B$ neither guarantees sparsity nor confers low computational cost automatically, nor does it establish an isomorphism with tropical (min-plus shortest-path) algebra**. On large-$N$ divisibility graphs, eliminating small integers of high degree links their multiples into dense cliques; whenever drop-threshold pruning is applied, one must jointly track the dropped-entry norm and the back-substituted residual $\lVert G\tilde{x} - b\rVert_2$ on the original system rather than claiming speedups from edge counts alone.

::: {.viz src="arithmetic-schur-lab" preset="drop" height="500"}
**Three-dimensional divisibility network under final Schur-block threshold pruning (`drop`).** After exact elimination onto the fixed terminals, the 3D divisibility network displays the magenta reduced couplings retained once off-diagonal entries of the final Schur block with $|S_{ij}| < \varepsilon$ are symmetrically zeroed while diagonal entries remain fixed, with the amber dot marking the currently selected vertex. This strategy prunes weak couplings only on the final terminal block and audits approximation accuracy through the Schur-block discrepancy and the original-system back-substitution residual.
:::

Conversely, one must not claim that repeated squaring on a divisibility graph inevitably triggers eigenvalue explosion. With step size $\tau = \frac{1}{1 + \max_{2 \le n \le N}\deg(n)}$ on the same divisibility graph, form the self-looped random-walk matrix

$$
P \;:=\; I - \tau L_D, \qquad \tau \;=\; \frac{1}{1 + \max\deg}.
$$

Because $L_D$ is real symmetric, $P$ is **real symmetric with a purely real spectrum**; simultaneously, this choice of $\tau$ keeps $P$ entrywise non-negative with every row summing to $1$, making $P$ a **symmetric non-negative row-stochastic matrix** whose eigenvalues lie in $(-1, 1]$ with spectral radius and spectral norm $\rho(P) = \lVert P\rVert_2 = 1 \le 1$. Applying $d$ sequential matrix-squaring layers yields $P^{2^d}$: **although there are only $d$ sequential matrix-squaring layers, the operator polynomial degree reaches $2^d$**, while $P^{2^d}$ always remains symmetric and entrywise non-negative row-stochastic with spectral radius $\rho(P^{2^d}) = 1 \le 1$ (at $d = 0$ it may still have negative eigenvalues, whereas for $d \ge 1$ its real spectrum is non-negative and bounded)—demonstrating that repeated squaring need not blow up spectrally, even though connected components rapidly fill in and their **fill density, multiply-add counts, and memory footprint** must still be accounted for.

::: {.viz src="arithmetic-schur-lab" preset="square" height="500"}
**Three-dimensional divisibility network under repeated squaring of the self-looped random walk (`square`).** Along purple coordinate axes, the 3D stage displays the integer divisibility network as the symmetric row-stochastic matrix $P = I - \tau L_D$ is squared layer by layer to $P^{2^d}$, with cyan edges marking the original divisibility adjacency. As the depth increases, magenta multistep couplings rapidly fill each non-isolated connected component while isolated vertices remain uncoupled; an amber dot on the purple scale marks the current squaring depth $d$.
:::

::: {.remark #rem-ch34-burnol-nbb-mollifier-literature title="Literature Boundaries on Burnol's Continuous-Dilation Lower Bound and the Mollifier Parameter 4/7 (Literature Results, Not Formalized Here)"}
**Literature results, not formalized in this book.** Two literature boundaries must be respected when comparing with analytic number theory: first, in $L^2(0, \infty)$, J.-F. Burnol (*A lower bound in an approximation problem involving the zeros of the Riemann zeta function*, [arXiv:math/0103058v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0103058v2), Theorem 1.3) defines $B_\lambda$ as the subspace of finite linear combinations of continuous fractional-part dilations $x \mapsto \{\theta / x\}$ with $\lambda \le \theta \le 1$, sets $D_\lambda := \operatorname{dist}(\chi, B_\lambda)$ for the NBB $L^2$ indicator target $\chi = 1_{(0, 1)}$, and proves the unconditional asymptotic lower bound

$$
\liminf_{\lambda \to 0^+} D_\lambda\sqrt{\log(1/\lambda)} \;\ge\; \sqrt{\sum_{\operatorname{Re}(\rho)=1/2} \frac{m_\rho^2}{|\rho|^2}}
$$

(where the right-hand sum with $m_\rho^2$ runs over **distinct** critical-line nontrivial zeros $\rho$ of multiplicity $m_\rho$; under the externally verified [@conj-riemann-hypothesis] together with Báez-Duarte's discrete criterion (see [@rem-ch34-arithmetic-pivot-literature]), the discrete distance satisfies $d_N \to 0$, and the subspace inclusion $D_{1/N} \le d_N$ together with monotonicity of $D_\lambda$ in $\lambda$ then yields the continuous limit $D_\lambda \to 0$; Burnol's lower bound above is independent of that convergence, and simplicity of all nontrivial zeros remains open). Note that Burnol's continuous-dilation subspace $B_\lambda$ **is not the same finite problem** as the discrete integer Gram system over $a = 1, \dots, N$; combining [@conj-riemann-hypothesis] with the hypothesis that all nontrivial zeros are simple reduces the sum inside the square root to $2 + \gamma - \log(4\pi) \approx 0.0461914$ (strictly larger if any zero has multiplicity $m_\rho > 1$), which does not yield an exact equality at arbitrary finite truncations. Second, the fraction $\frac{4}{7}$ is the logarithmic length ratio of a specific Dirichlet-polynomial **mollifier** in critical-line zero-proportion estimates rather than a universal physical tunneling barrier, and M. Radziwiłł (*Limitations to mollifying $\zeta(s)$*, [arXiv:1207.6583v3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.6583v3), §1 and Theorem 1) explicitly distinguishes specific finite-scale mollifier bounds from arbitrarily long mollifiers.

Moreover, the finite-dimensional span generated by discrete dilations $\theta = 1/n$ for $1 \le n \le M$ is only a subspace of the continuous space $B_{1/M}$, so the two are not the same finite approximation problem and the continuous-subspace distance $D_{1/M}$ must not be equated with the discrete approximation distance; under [@conj-riemann-hypothesis] and the two-sided zeros convention, simplicity of all nontrivial zeros yields $\liminf_{M \to \infty} D_{1/M}^2 \log M \ge 2 + \gamma - \log(4\pi) \approx 0.0461914$ (and the right-hand side is at least that constant for general multiplicities), so this constant is an asymptotic $\liminf$ lower bound for $D_{1/M}^2 \log M$ as $M \to \infty$, not an equality at finite $M$.
:::

Before passing from continuous subspaces to discrete, localized arithmetic approximations, one spatial-side fact is fundamental: **the genuine Nyman–Beurling Gram matrix is dense from the outset**. For any positive integers $a, b \ge 1$, set $f_a(x) := \{1/(ax)\}$, where $\{u\} = u - \lfloor u\rfloor \in [0, 1)$. Since the integrand is strictly positive almost everywhere on $\bigl(0, \frac{1}{\min(a,b)}\bigr)$ and $x > \frac{1}{\min(a,b)}$ implies $0 < \frac{1}{ax}, \frac{1}{bx} < 1$ so $f_a(x) = \frac{1}{ax}$ and $f_b(x) = \frac{1}{bx}$, dropping the strictly positive contribution on $\bigl(0, \frac{1}{\min(a,b)}\bigr)$ gives the following strict entrywise bound, whose right-hand side is at least $\frac{1}{ab}$

$$
G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{ab} \;=\; \int_0^\infty \left\{\frac{1}{ax}\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{bx}\right\}dx \;>\; \int_{1/\min(a,b)}^\infty \frac{dx}{ab\,x^2} \;=\; \frac{1}{\max(a, b)} \;>\; 0.
$$

Because each fractional-part kernel carries a hyperbolic tail $1/(ax)$ at infinity, every pair of basis dilations has positive overlap on $\bigl(\frac{1}{\min(a,b)}, \infty\bigr)$: unlike sparse graph matrices whose fill-in arises only after shared hubs are eliminated, $G^{\mathrm{NB}}$ is fully dense before the first pivot, with no structural zeros to preserve. The next remark establishes the exact closed form, unconditional two-sided polynomial spectral and condition-number bounds, and 65-digit counterexamples refuting fixed-threshold Schur pruning on the genuine matrix $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ itself.

::: {.remark #rem-ch34-nbb-gram-spectral-and-pruning title="Exact Closed Form, Unconditional Two-Sided Polynomial Spectral Bounds, and Fixed-Threshold Schur-Pruning Counterexamples for the Genuine NBB Gram Matrix (Manual derivation, literature result, and high-precision numerical check)"}
**Provenance and verification status.** For $N \ge 1$, let $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N := \bigl[\langle f_a, f_b\rangle_{L^2(0,\infty)}\bigr]_{a,b=1}^N$ denote the genuine Nyman–Beurling–Báez-Duarte (NBB) Gram matrix, $C := \ln(2\pi) - \gamma \approx 1.2606614015$, $H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N := D_N G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N D_N$ the diagonally normalized correlation matrix with $(D_N)_{aa} := \sqrt{a / C}$ (so $(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N)_{aa} = 1$), $H_N := \sum_{k=1}^N \frac{1}{k}$ the harmonic number, and $b_a = \langle f_a, \chi\rangle = \frac{\ln a + 1 - \gamma}{a}$ the load vector (frozen in [`fractional_reciprocal_inner_product`](lean:D5/S3/Constants/InnerProducts/FractionalReciprocalInnerProduct.fractional_reciprocal_inner_product), with finite distance formula $d_N^2 = 1 - b^\top (G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N)^{-1} b$ in [@prop-ch34-nyman-beurling-frozen-facts] and [`nyman_beurling_finite_gram_distance`](lean:D5/S3/Observer/Hilbert/NymanBeurlingFiniteGramDistance.nyman_beurling_finite_gram_distance)). Vasyunin's closed form in item 1 is a classical literature result (also cited in [@ex-ch34-nyman-beurling-numbers]); the two-sided spectral bounds, condition-number exponents, exact $L^2$ residual perturbation identities, and fixed-threshold pruning counterexamples in items 2–4 are unconditional manual derivations and 65- to 82-digit numerical verifications (independent of [@conj-riemann-hypothesis]).

1. **Vasyunin closed form and Mellin–Plancherel Bochner representation (literature result $+$ manual derivation)**: Write $d = \gcd(a, b)$, $p = a / d$, $q = b / d$, and define the Vasyunin cotangent sum $V(p, q) := \sum_{k=1}^{q-1} \bigl\{\frac{kp}{q}\bigr\}\cot\frac{\pi k}{q}$ (empty sum $0$ when $q = 1$). By V. I. Vasyunin (1995), L. Báez-Duarte, M. Balazard, B. Landreau, and E. Saias (*Étude de l'autocorrélation multiplicative de la fonction « partie fractionnaire »*, *Ramanujan J.* **9**, 215–240, 2005, [10.1007/s11139-005-0834-4](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11139-005-0834-4); working paper [arXiv:math/0306251v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0306251v1), Propositions 87 and 89), and S. Bettin and J. B. Conrey (*Period functions and cotangent sums*, *Algebra Number Theory* **7**, 215–242, 2013, [10.2140/ant.2013.7.215](https://doi.org/10.2140/ant.2013.7.215), Eq. (13)), every pair of positive integers $(a, b)$ satisfies the exact scaling law $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{da, db} = \frac{1}{d} G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{a, b}$, diagonal formula $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{aa} = \frac{C}{a}$, and finite elementary closed form
   $$
   G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{ab} \;=\; \frac{C(a + b) + (a - b)\ln(b/a) - \pi d\,\bigl[V(p, q) + V(q, p)\bigr]}{2ab}.
   $$
   In parallel, adding the elementary $(1, \infty)$ tail $\int_1^\infty \frac{\theta}{x}x^{s-1}\,dx = -\frac{\theta}{s-1}$ ($0 < \operatorname{Re} s < 1$) to the frozen Mellin identity [`fractionalMellin_eq_zeta`](lean:D5/S3/Weil/ZetaPntBounds/NymanFractionalMellin.fractionalMellin_eq_zeta) ($\int_0^1 \{\theta/x\}x^{s-1}\,dx = \frac{\theta}{s-1} - \frac{\theta^s\zeta(s)}{s}$ for $0 < \theta \le 1$ and $0 < \operatorname{Re} s < 1$, applied here at $\theta = 1/a \in (0, 1]$) cancels the pole term identically and gives $(\mathcal{M} f_a)(s) = -a^{-s}\zeta(s)/s$; Mellin–Plancherel isometry on $L^2(0,\infty)$ then yields the positive-density Bochner representation (manual derivation):
   $$
   G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{ab} \;=\; \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty} \frac{\lvert\zeta(\frac{1}{2} + it)\rvert^2}{\frac{1}{4} + t^2}\,a^{-\frac{1}{2} - it}\,b^{-\frac{1}{2} + it}\,dt,
   $$
   so the scaled kernel $\sqrt{ab}\,G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{ab}$ depends solely on the logarithmic ratio $\ln(a/b)$. Across all $78$ upper-triangular entries $(a, b)$ with $1 \le a \le b \le 12$ (including the diagonal), Vasyunin's closed form agrees with 82-digit piecewise trigamma quadrature $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{ab} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^1 \{Lt/a\}\{Lt/b\}\psi_1(t)\,dt$ ($L = \operatorname{lcm}(a, b)$) to within $2.48\times 10^{-83}$, and an independent 132-term interval Taylor certificate of $\psi_1(1 + t)$ encloses every entry to width below $1.89\times 10^{-61}$ (`NUM`).

2. **Unconditional two-sided polynomial spectral bounds and quadratic condition-number law (manual derivation, independent of RH)**: For every $N \ge 2$, let $L_N := \bigl\lVert N f_N - (N - 1) f_{N-1}\bigr\rVert_{L^2(0,\infty)}^2$ denote the squared norm of the adjacent dilation difference. Then $L_N$ satisfies the exact closed form and asymptotic expansion
   $$
   L_N \;=\; \ln\frac{N}{N - 1} + \pi\bigl[V(1, N - 1) - V(1, N)\bigr] \;=\; \ln N + 1 - C + \frac{1}{2N} + O\!\left(\frac{1}{N^2}\right),
   $$
   and **for every $N \ge 2$**, both $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ and $H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ are unconditionally strictly positive definite and obey the explicit two-sided eigenvalue bounds:
   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   \frac{1}{36 N^2 H_N^2} &\;\le\; \lambda_{\min}\bigl(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N\bigr) \;\le\; \frac{L_N}{N^2 + (N - 1)^2}, \qquad &
   C &\;\le\; \lambda_{\max}\bigl(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N\bigr) \;\le\; C H_N, \\
   \frac{1}{36 C N H_N^2} &\;\le\; \lambda_{\min}\bigl(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N\bigr) \;\le\; \frac{L_N}{C(2N - 1)}, \qquad &
   \frac{2N - 1}{3C} &\;\le\; \lambda_{\max}\bigl(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N\bigr) \;\le\; N.
   \end{aligned}
   $$
   These pin down the exact polynomial exponents $\lambda_{\min}(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) = N^{-2 + o(1)}$ and $\lambda_{\min}(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) = N^{-1 + o(1)}$; although diagonal scaling raises the minimal eigenvalue from $N^{-2 + o(1)}$ to $N^{-1 + o(1)}$ (a factor of $N^{1 + o(1)}$), it simultaneously raises the maximal eigenvalue from the interval $[C, C H_N]$ (order $N^{o(1)}$) to $\Theta(N)$, so **both $2$-condition numbers grow with quadratic polynomial exponent $N^{2 + o(1)}$**:
   $$
   \frac{C\bigl(N^2 + (N - 1)^2\bigr)}{L_N} \;\le\; \kappa_2\bigl(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N\bigr) \;\le\; 36 C N^2 H_N^3, \qquad
   \frac{(2N - 1)^2}{3 L_N} \;\le\; \kappa_2\bigl(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N\bigr) \;\le\; 36 C N^2 H_N^2,
   $$
   hence $\kappa_2(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) = N^{2 + o(1)}$ and $\kappa_2(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) = N^{2 + o(1)}$.

   **Proof.** *Lower bounds*: For any $c = (c_1, \dots, c_N) \in \mathbb{C}^N$, set $g(u) := \sum_{a=1}^N c_a \{u/a\}$; substituting $u = 1/x$ gives $c^* G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N c = \int_0^\infty \lvert g(u)\rvert^2 \frac{du}{u^2}$. Each open unit interval $(j - 1, j)$ ($j \in \mathbb{Z}_{\ge 1}$) contains no integers, so every $\{u/a\}$ is affine on $(j - 1, j)$ and hence $g$ is affine there, with downward jump $J_j := g(j^-) - g(j^+) = \sum_{a \mid j} c_a$ at integer $j \ge 1$. For any complex affine function $\ell(t) = \ell(0)(1 - t) + \ell(1)t$ on $[0, 1]$, direct expansion gives the exact square identities $4\int_0^1 \lvert\ell(t)\rvert^2\,dt - \lvert\ell(0)\rvert^2 = \frac{1}{3}\lvert\ell(0) + 2\ell(1)\rvert^2 \ge 0$ and $4\int_0^1 \lvert\ell(t)\rvert^2\,dt - \lvert\ell(1)\rvert^2 = \frac{1}{3}\lvert 2\ell(0) + \ell(1)\rvert^2 \ge 0$. On $(j - 1, j)$ one has $\frac{1}{j^2} \le \frac{1}{u^2}$, so $\frac{\lvert g(j^-)\rvert^2}{j^2} \le 4\int_{j-1}^j \lvert g(u)\rvert^2\frac{du}{u^2}$; on $(j, j + 1)$ one has $u \le j + 1 \le 2j$, i.e. $\frac{1}{j^2} \le \frac{4}{u^2}$, so $\frac{\lvert g(j^+)\rvert^2}{j^2} \le 16\int_j^{j+1}\lvert g(u)\rvert^2\frac{du}{u^2}$. Applying the exact identity $3\lvert z\rvert^2 + \frac{3}{2}\lvert w\rvert^2 - \lvert z - w\rvert^2 = \lvert\sqrt{2}z + \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}w\rvert^2 \ge 0$ yields
   $$
   \frac{\lvert J_j\rvert^2}{j^2} \;\le\; 12\int_{j-1}^j \lvert g(u)\rvert^2\frac{du}{u^2} + 24\int_j^{j+1} \lvert g(u)\rvert^2\frac{du}{u^2}.
   $$
   Summing over $j = 1, \dots, N$ (each interior interval appears at most twice, with total weight $12 + 24 = 36$) gives $\sum_{j=1}^N \frac{\lvert J_j\rvert^2}{j^2} \le 36\int_0^{N+1}\lvert g(u)\rvert^2\frac{du}{u^2} \le 36\,c^* G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N c$. Applying Möbius inversion $c_a = \sum_{k \mid a} \mu(k) J_{a/k}$, dividing by $a$, and setting $v_j := J_j / j$ together with the $\ell^2$-contractive dilation operators $(D_k v)_a := v_{a/k}$ for $k \mid a$ and $0$ otherwise ($\lVert D_k v\rVert_2 \le \lVert v\rVert_2$), the triangle inequality gives
   $$
   \left(\sum_{a=1}^N \frac{\lvert c_a\rvert^2}{a^2}\right)^{1/2} \;=\; \left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^N \frac{\mu(k)}{k} D_k v\right\rVert_2 \;\le\; \left(\sum_{k=1}^N \frac{\lvert\mu(k)\rvert}{k}\right)\lVert v\rVert_2 \;\le\; H_N \lVert v\rVert_2.
   $$
   Squaring yields $\sum_{a=1}^N \frac{\lvert c_a\rvert^2}{a^2} \le 36 H_N^2\, c^* G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N c$. Using $\frac{1}{N^2}\lVert c\rVert_2^2 \le \sum_{a=1}^N \frac{\lvert c_a\rvert^2}{a^2}$ proves $\lambda_{\min}(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) \ge \frac{1}{36 N^2 H_N^2}$; setting $c_a = \sqrt{a}\,x_a$ and using $x^* H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N x = \frac{1}{C}c^* G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N c$ with $\frac{1}{N}\lVert x\rVert_2^2 \le \sum_{a=1}^N \frac{\lvert c_a\rvert^2}{a^2}$ proves $\lambda_{\min}(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) \ge \frac{1}{36 C N H_N^2}$.

   *Upper bounds and maximal eigenvalues*: Because $N\{u/N\} - (N - 1)\{u/(N - 1)\} \equiv 0$ identically on $(0, N - 1)$, expanding $L_N = N^2 G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{NN} + (N - 1)^2 G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{N-1,N-1} - 2N(N - 1)G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{N-1,N}$ via Vasyunin's formula and using $\gcd(N - 1, N) = 1$, $V(N - 1, N) = -V(1, N)$ (from $\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\cot\frac{\pi k}{N} = 0$), and $V(N, N - 1) = V(1, N - 1)$ yields $L_N = \ln\frac{N}{N - 1} + \pi\bigl[V(1, N - 1) - V(1, N)\bigr]$. Subtracting the endpoint pole $-\frac{1}{\pi(1 - x)}$ from $x\cot(\pi x)$ and applying Euler–Maclaurin to the smooth function $h(x) := x\cot(\pi x) + \frac{1}{\pi(1 - x)}$ (with $\int_0^1 h(x)\,dx = \frac{\ln(2\pi)}{\pi}$, $h(0) = \frac{2}{\pi}$, $h(1) = \frac{1}{\pi}$, $h'(0) = \frac{1}{\pi}$, $h'(1) = -\frac{\pi}{3}$) gives $\pi V(1, n) = -n(\ln n - C) - 1 - \frac{\pi^2}{36n} + O(n^{-3})$, whence $L_N = \ln N + 1 - C + \frac{1}{2N} + O(N^{-2})$. Evaluating Rayleigh quotients at $u_N := (N e_N - (N - 1)e_{N-1})/\sqrt{N^2 + (N - 1)^2}$ for $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ and $(\sqrt{N} e_N - \sqrt{N - 1}e_{N-1})/\sqrt{2N - 1}$ for $H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ yields both $\lambda_{\min}$ upper bounds. For $\lambda_{\max}$: $C = G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{1,1} \le \lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) \le \operatorname{tr}(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) = C H_N$; from $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{ab} \ge \frac{1}{\max(a, b)}$ we have $H^{\mathrm{NB}}_{ab} \ge \frac{1}{C}\sqrt{\frac{\min(a,b)}{\max(a,b)}}$, so the unit all-ones vector $\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbf{1}_N$ together with $\sum_{a=1}^b \sqrt{a} \ge \int_0^b \sqrt{t}\,dt = \frac{2}{3}b^{3/2}$ gives $\lambda_{\max}(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) \ge \frac{1}{NC}\bigl(2\sum_{b=1}^N \frac{2}{3}b - N\bigr) = \frac{2N - 1}{3C}$, while $\lambda_{\max}(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) \le \operatorname{tr}(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N) = N$. $\square$

3. **Exact $L^2(0,\infty)$ residual perturbation identity under symmetric approximation (manual derivation)**: Suppose any compression or approximate Schur-elimination scheme replaces $G := G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ by a real symmetric approximation $\widetilde{G} = G + E$ with $\lVert E\rVert_2 < \lambda_{\min}(G)$ (ensuring $\widetilde{G} \succ 0$). Denote the approximate coefficients by $\tilde{c} := \widetilde{G}^{-1} b$, the surrogate residual by $\widetilde{d}_N^2 := 1 - b^\top \widetilde{G}^{-1} b$, and the **true $L^2(0,\infty)$ squared residual** induced by $\tilde{c}$ by $d_{\mathrm{true}}^2(\tilde{c}) := \bigl\lVert\chi - \sum_{a=1}^N \tilde{c}_a f_a\bigr\rVert_{L^2(0,\infty)}^2 = d_N^2 + (\tilde{c} - G^{-1}b)^\top G(\tilde{c} - G^{-1}b)$. From $G(\tilde{c} - G^{-1}b) = -E\tilde{c}$ and the resolvent identity $\widetilde{G}^{-1} - G^{-1} = -G^{-1} E \widetilde{G}^{-1}$, one obtains the exact excess identity and bounds
   $$
   d_{\mathrm{true}}^2(\tilde{c}) - d_N^2 \;=\; \bigl\lVert G^{-1/2} E \widetilde{G}^{-1} b\bigr\rVert_2^2 \;\le\; \frac{\lVert E\rVert_2^2\,\lVert\tilde{c}\rVert_2^2}{\lambda_{\min}(G)}, \qquad
   \bigl\lvert\widetilde{d}_N^2 - d_N^2\bigr\rvert \;\le\; \frac{\lVert E\rVert_2\,\lVert b\rVert_2^2}{\lambda_{\min}(G)\bigl(\lambda_{\min}(G) - \lVert E\rVert_2\bigr)}.
   $$
   Combined with the upper bound $\lambda_{\min}(G) \le \frac{L_N}{N^2 + (N - 1)^2} = O\!\left(\frac{\ln N}{N^2}\right)$ from item 2, the sufficient condition $\lVert E\rVert_2 < \lambda_{\min}(G)$ for guaranteed positive definiteness and certified $L^2$ residual control requires $\lVert E\rVert_2$ to stay strictly below $\lambda_{\min}(G)$—and in particular forces $\lVert E\rVert_2 < \frac{L_N}{N^2 + (N - 1)^2} = O\!\left(\frac{\ln N}{N^2}\right)$ (for instance, item 2's lower bound shows $\lVert E\rVert_2 < \frac{1}{36 N^2 H_N^2}$ always suffices)—so any fixed-threshold pruning rule that incurs constant-order spectral error exits this sufficient regime as $N$ grows; concrete breakdowns of positive definiteness for genuine $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ and $H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ are established directly by the explicit counterexamples in item 4 below.

4. **Spectra of genuine $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$, fixed-threshold Schur-pruning counterexamples, and off-diagonal singular spectra (`NUM`, 65-digit arithmetic cross-checked against float64)**:
   Table entries below are rounded from 65-digit computations (relative eigenvalue discrepancy vs. float64 $< 1.6\times 10^{-13}$, $d_N^2$ discrepancy $< 4.2\times 10^{-16}$):

   | $N$ | $\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N)$ | $\lambda_{\min}(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N)$ | $\kappa_2(G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N)$ | $\lambda_{\min}(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N)$ | $\kappa_2(H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N)$ | $d_N^2$ | $(\ln N)\,d_N^2$ | Quotient $u_N^\top G u_N / \lambda_{\min}(G)$ |
   | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
   | **$2$** | $1.77954$ | $1.11448\times 10^{-1}$ | $15.968$ | $1.33733\times 10^{-1}$ | $13.955$ | $0.289965$ | $0.200988$ | $1.244$ |
   | **$8$** | $2.85453$ | $1.42229\times 10^{-2}$ | $200.70$ | $6.51743\times 10^{-2}$ | $102.34$ | $0.024161$ | $0.050242$ | $1.171$ |
   | **$32$** | $3.81823$ | $9.97655\times 10^{-4}$ | $3827.2$ | $1.84249\times 10^{-2}$ | $1388.9$ | $0.014052$ | $0.048700$ | $1.626$ |
   | **$64$** | $4.23451$ | $2.80096\times 10^{-4}$ | $15118$ | $1.01399\times 10^{-2}$ | $5009.0$ | $0.011376$ | $0.047312$ | $1.729$ |
   | **$128$** | $4.60766$ | $7.26460\times 10^{-5}$ | $63426$ | $5.44004\times 10^{-3}$ | $18600$ | $0.009659$ | $0.046864$ | $1.946$ |

   *Counterexample tests of leaf-first divisibility-order absolute-threshold Schur pruning on genuine $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ and $H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$*: Applying the order that eliminates the NBB index $1$ first and then follows this section's divisibility graph on $\{2,\dots,N\}$ (updating fill on that graph, selecting the largest index on minimum-degree ties, and retaining the terminal block $4a > 3N$) to genuine $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ and $H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$, we tested fixed absolute off-diagonal pruning at $\tau \in \{10^{-2}, 10^{-3}, 10^{-4}, 10^{-5}\}$ on the raw matrix, on the terminal Schur block, and after every pivot (dropping symmetric off-diagonal entries of magnitude below $\tau$ and preserving diagonals; 65-digit recomputation agrees with float64 residuals within $2.6\times 10^{-12}$):
   - **Tested fixed absolute thresholds break positive definiteness and blow up the true squared residual**: At $N = 64$, terminal-block pruning at $\tau = 10^{-3}$ incurs relative spectral error only $\lVert E\rVert_2 / \lVert G\rVert_2 = 9.06\times 10^{-4}$ yet introduces **$2$ negative eigenvalues** ($\lambda_{\min} = -3.81\times 10^{-4}$); at $N = 128$, terminal-block pruning at $\tau = 10^{-3}$ creates **$7$ negative eigenvalues** ($\lambda_{\min} = -1.49\times 10^{-3}$) and pushes the true squared residual $d_{\mathrm{true}}^2$ from the optimal baseline $d_{128}^2 \approx 0.009659$ up to $0.018048$; and per-pivot pruning of raw $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{128}$ at $\tau = 10^{-4}$ has relative spectral error only **$0.060\%$** ($6.00\times 10^{-4}$), yet generates **$14$ negative eigenvalues** ($\lambda_{\min} = -3.72\times 10^{-4}$) and inflates the true squared residual $d_{\mathrm{true}}^2$ to **$3.5390$ ($366\times$ the optimal squared residual, i.e., $19.1\times$ in $L^2$ norm)**! Even after diagonal scaling to $H^{\mathrm{NB}}_{128}$, per-pivot pruning at $\tau = 10^{-2}$ yields **$20$ negative eigenvalues** ($d_{\mathrm{true}}^2 = 2.110$), and at $\tau = 10^{-3}$—while remaining positive definite—still doubles the true $L^2$ squared residual to $0.01936$. These counterexamples refute positive definiteness and controlled $L^2$ residuals for the tested elimination order and fixed thresholds ($\tau = 10^{-2}$ to $10^{-4}$); the admissible spectral-error ceiling in the sufficient certificate of item 3 shrinks with $N$.
   - **Off-diagonal singular spectra and Vasyunin residual cancellation**: Splitting $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{ab}$ into its rank-$\le 4$ smooth part $K^{\mathrm{smooth}}_{ab} := \frac{C(a+b)+(a-b)\ln(b/a)}{2ab}$ and Vasyunin cotangent residual $R_{ab} := -\frac{\pi d}{2ab}[V(p,q)+V(q,p)]$, 65–70-digit SVD checks at $N = 64, 128, 256$ on separated ($\{1,\dots,N/4\} \times \{3N/4+1,\dots,N\}$) and adjacent ($\{1,\dots,N/2\} \times \{N/2+1,\dots,N\}$) off-diagonal blocks (cross-checked against float64; for example, the maximum entrywise relative singular-value discrepancy between float64 and 70-digit arithmetic on the $N = 64$ separated block is $1.09\times 10^{-12}$) show that although the off-diagonal singular values of $G^{\mathrm{NB}}$ decay with index and reveal no flat arithmetic floor up to $N = 256$, strong cancellation occurs between $K^{\mathrm{smooth}}$ and $R$: at $N = 256$, $\lVert R\rVert_2$ is **$29.1\times$ (separated) and $19.9\times$ (adjacent)** the original block norm $\lVert G^{\mathrm{NB}}\rVert_2$ ($7.21\times$ and $3.75\times$ for $D R D$). At a common tolerance $10^{-4}\lVert G_{\text{block}}\rVert_2$, the numerical ranks of $G^{\mathrm{NB}}$ grow steadily across $N = 64 \to 128 \to 256$ ($12 \to 19 \to 23$ separated; $24 \to 39 \to 42$ adjacent), and the numerical ranks of $R$ track them closely ($11$ vs. $12$ separated at $N = 64$, $38$ vs. $39$ adjacent at $N = 128$, and matching exactly at $N = 256$ with $23$ separated and $42$ adjacent); [@rem-ch34-nbb-subcubic-spectral-solver] below contrasts this growth against fixed-band spectral-cutoff rank plateaus under each block's own relative threshold and constructs a fixed-tolerance positive-definite subcubic solver directly.
:::

::: {.remark #rem-ch34-nbb-subcubic-spectral-solver title="Fixed-Tolerance Positive-Definite Subcubic Mellin–Plancherel Spectral-Quadrature Solver and Dual Computable $L^2$ Residual Certificates for Genuine $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ (Manual Derivation, Literature, and High-Precision Arb/FLINT Verification)"}
**Provenance and verification status.** Building directly on the same frozen declarations cited in [@rem-ch34-nbb-gram-spectral-and-pruning]—[`fractionalMellin_eq_zeta`](lean:D5/S3/Weil/ZetaPntBounds/NymanFractionalMellin.fractionalMellin_eq_zeta), [`fractional_reciprocal_inner_product`](lean:D5/S3/Constants/InnerProducts/FractionalReciprocalInnerProduct.fractional_reciprocal_inner_product), and [`nyman_beurling_finite_gram_distance`](lean:D5/S3/Observer/Hilbert/NymanBeurlingFiniteGramDistance.nyman_beurling_finite_gram_distance)—this remark constructs a matrix-free Mellin–Plancherel spectral-quadrature / Taylor–FFT solver for genuine $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ whose exact surrogate stays strictly positive definite and is equipped with computable $L^2(0, \infty)$ residual certificates. At fixed error tolerance $\tau \in (0, 1]$ (full variable-tolerance scaling is detailed in item 3 and [@prob-ch34-arithmetic-schur-subcubic-question]), its unconditional track is completely independent of [@conj-riemann-hypothesis] (breaking the $N^3$ upper-bound barrier via explicit Weyl, explicit sub-Weyl, and Bourgain subconvexity bounds), while its RH/Lindelöf track explicitly invokes the external headline theorem `riemannZeta_eq_zero_re : RiemannZetaZerosOnLine` recorded in [@conj-riemann-hypothesis] to reduce fixed-tolerance arithmetic time to quasi-quadratic $O_\delta(N^{2+\delta})$ and storage to near-linear $O_\delta(N^{1+\delta})$. The analytic derivations and FLINT/Arb interval checks up to $4{,}200$-bit precision (`manual derivation` / `NUM`, not formalized in this book; full benchmark log and reproducibility addendum in repository [Issue #893 main log](https://github.com/the-omega-institute/trureturing-books/issues/893#issuecomment-7072121051) and [addendum](https://github.com/the-omega-institute/trureturing-books/issues/893#issuecomment-7072893626)) proceed in four steps:

1. **Mellin–Plancherel spectral representation, analyticity strip, and Montgomery–Vaughan dual tail-norm bounds**: Set $D := \operatorname{diag}(1, 2, \dots, N)$, $A_N := D^{1/2} G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N D^{1/2} = C H^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ ($C = \ln(2\pi) - \gamma$), and $\tilde{b} := D^{1/2} b$, so $\tilde{b}_a = \frac{\ln a + 1 - \gamma}{\sqrt{a}}$. The original linear system $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N c = b$ is equivalent to $A_N x = \tilde{b}$ under the **exact coordinate conversion $c_a = \sqrt{a}\,x_a$ ($x_a = c_a / \sqrt{a}$)**, preserving $c^\top G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N c = x^\top A_N x$ and $b^\top c = \tilde{b}^\top x$. Combining $(\mathcal{M} f_a)(s) = -a^{-s}\zeta(s)/s$ from item 1 of [@rem-ch34-nbb-gram-spectral-and-pruning] with $(\mathcal{M}\chi)(s) = 1/s$, Mellin–Plancherel isometry gives

   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   (A_N)_{ab} &\;=\; \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty} w(t)\,e^{it\ln(b/a)}\,dt, \qquad w(t) \;:=\; \frac{\lvert\zeta(\frac{1}{2} + it)\rvert^2}{\frac{1}{4} + t^2} \;\ge\; 0, \\
   \tilde{b}_a &\;=\; -\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty} \frac{\zeta(\frac{1}{2} + it)\,a^{-it}}{\frac{1}{4} + t^2}\,dt
   \end{aligned}
   $$

   (Arb contour integrals at $a \in \{1, 2, 32, 128\}$ verified both the minus sign and the closed form with real-part enclosure radii below $5\times 10^{-33}$ and zero-containing imaginary enclosures). The meromorphic continuation $w(z) := \frac{\zeta(\frac{1}{2} + iz)\zeta(\frac{1}{2} - iz)}{(\frac{1}{2} + iz)(\frac{1}{2} - iz)}$ is holomorphic throughout the horizontal strip $\lvert\operatorname{Im} z\rvert < \frac{1}{2}$, with double poles on its boundary at $z = \pm\frac{i}{2}$ (leading Laurent term $-\frac{1}{2(z \mp i/2)^2}$); on every closed substrip $\lvert\operatorname{Im} z\rvert \le \sigma_0 < \frac{1}{2}$, writing $\sigma_\pm = \frac{1}{2} \mp \operatorname{Im} z$ yields $\frac{1 - \sigma_+}{2} + \frac{1 - \sigma_-}{2} \equiv \frac{1}{2}$, so Phragmén–Lindelöf convexity gives the **height-independent integrable decay** $w(z) = O_{\sigma_0}\bigl((1 + \lvert z\rvert)^{-3/2}\ln^2(2 + \lvert z\rvert)\bigr)$.

   Splitting $A_N = A^{(T)}_N + E^{(T)}_N$ at frequency cutoff $T > 0$ ($\lvert t\rvert \le T$ vs. $\lvert t\rvert > T$) makes both pieces exact positive semidefinite matrices ($A^{(T)}_N \succeq 0$, $E^{(T)}_N \succeq 0$). The logarithmic frequencies $\lambda_a = \ln a$ ($1 \le a \le N$, $N \ge 2$) satisfy $\delta_a := \min_{b \neq a}\lvert\ln a - \ln b\rvert = \ln(1 + 1/a) > \frac{1}{a+1} \ge \frac{1}{N}$ for $a < N$ and $\delta_N = \ln\frac{N}{N-1} > \frac{1}{N}$, hence $\delta_{\min} := \min_a \delta_a > \frac{1}{N}$. Applying the uniform Hilbert inequality of H. L. Montgomery & R. C. Vaughan (*Hilbert's inequality*, *J. London Math. Soc.* (2) **8**(1), 73–82, 1974, [10.1112/jlms/s2-8.1.73](https://doi.org/10.1112/jlms/s2-8.1.73), whose uniform spacing constant $\pi$ gives $\int_U^{U+H}\bigl\lvert\sum_{a=1}^N x_a a^{-it}\bigr\rvert^2 dt \le (H + 2\pi/\delta_{\min})\lVert x\rVert_2^2 \le (H + 2\pi N)\lVert x\rVert_2^2$ for all $x \in \mathbb{C}^N$, strict for $x \neq 0$; for the individual-spacing constant $\frac{3\pi}{2}$ and Preissmann's refinement see also W. Yangjit, *On the Montgomery–Vaughan weighted generalization of Hilbert's inequality*, *Proc. Amer. Math. Soc. Ser. B* **10**, 439–454, 2023, [10.1090/bproc/199](https://doi.org/10.1090/bproc/199), preprint [arXiv:2203.14950v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.14950v1), eqs. (1.2), (1.3), and (1.5)) and summing over dyadic shells $[2^k T, 2^{k+1} T]$ ($k \ge 0$, both signs of $t$), any pointwise bound $\lvert\zeta(\frac{1}{2} + it)\rvert \le A_0 \lvert t\rvert^\beta$ on $\lvert t\rvert \ge T \ge 1$ with $s := 1 - 2\beta > 0$ implies the explicit operator-norm tail certificate

   $$
   \lVert E^{(T)}_N\rVert_2 \;\le\; \frac{A_0^2}{\pi T^s}\!\left(\frac{1}{1 - 2^{-s}} \;+\; \frac{2\pi N / T}{1 - 2^{-s-1}}\right)
   $$

   (multiplying the $k$-th dyadic summand by $(\ln(2T) + k\ln 2)^2$ when a $\ln\lvert t\rvert$ factor is present), yielding $\lVert E^{(T)}_N\rVert_2 = O_\delta\bigl(T^{-(1-2\mu)+\delta}(1 + N/T)\bigr)$ for any exponent $\mu \in [0, \frac{1}{4}]$ with $\lvert\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+it)\rvert \ll_\delta (1+\lvert t\rvert)^{\mu+\delta}$. Separately, at **fixed $N$** as $T \to \infty$, specializing the twisted second-moment theorem of R. Balasubramanian, J. B. Conrey & D. R. Heath-Brown (*Asymptotic mean square of the product of the Riemann zeta-function and a Dirichlet polynomial*, *J. Reine Angew. Math.* **357**, 161–181, 1985, §1, Theorem 1, estimate (A), p. 161, [10.1515/crll.1985.357.161](https://doi.org/10.1515/crll.1985.357.161)) to $a(n) = \sqrt{n}\,x_n$ and integrating by parts against $(\frac{1}{4}+t^2)^{-1}$ across both tails yields the exact asymptotic

   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   \lVert E^{(T)}_N\rVert_2 &\;=\; \frac{\lambda_{\max}(B_N)}{\pi}\,\frac{\ln T}{T} \;+\; O_N(T^{-1}), \\
   (B_N)_{ab} &\;:=\; \frac{\gcd(a, b)}{\sqrt{ab}}
   \end{aligned}
   $$

   (for instance, at $T = 400$ the measured ratios $\lVert E^{(T)}_N\rVert_2 / (\frac{\ln T}{T})$ for $N = 16, 32, 64, 128$ are $1.32798, 1.63191, 1.94255, 2.26992$, approaching the fixed-$N$ limits $\lambda_{\max}(B_N)/\pi \approx 1.67923, 2.25541, 2.95914, 3.78925$).

2. **Composite Gauss–Legendre quadrature, real symmetric PSD Taylor–FFT factorization, and end-to-end finite-precision Chebyshev budget**: Choose panel width cap $h := \min\bigl(\frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{\ln N}\bigr)$, half-axis panel count $M := \lceil T / h\rceil = \Theta(1 + T\ln N)$ (simplifying to $\Theta(T\ln N)$ for $T \ge 1$), and exact panel width $H := T / M \le h$, placing all scaled points $y_a := H\ln a \in [0, 1] \subset [0, 2\pi)$ without wrap-around aliasing. On each panel $[mH, (m+1)H]$ ($m = -M, \dots, M - 1$), apply $p$-point Gauss–Legendre quadrature with nodes $t_{m,j} = H(m + \xi_j)$ ($\xi_j \in (0, 1)$), panel-scaled Gauss–Legendre quadrature weights $\alpha_{m,j} > 0$ ($\sum_{j=1}^p \alpha_{m,j} = H$), and non-negative spectral weights $\omega_{m,j} := \frac{w(t_{m,j})\alpha_{m,j}}{2\pi} \ge 0$ (vanishing only if a node coincides with a critical-line zero of $\zeta$), forming the **manifestly real symmetric positive semidefinite** spectral surrogate (symmetric node pairing keeps $\widehat{A}_N$ real; strict positive definiteness follows not from $2M \ge N$ alone nor from a bound on $S_{\mathbb{R}}$ alone, but automatically whenever its own perturbation satisfies $\lVert A_N - \widehat{A}_N\rVert_2 \le L/2$, in particular under the split budget of item 3)

   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   \widehat{A}_N &\;:=\; \sum_{j=1}^{p} \bigl(V^{(j)}\bigr)^* \operatorname{diag}\bigl(\omega_{\cdot,j}\bigr)\,V^{(j)} \;\succeq\; 0, \\
   V^{(j)}_{m,a} &\;=\; e^{-i m y_a}\,e^{-i\xi_j y_a}.
   \end{aligned}
   $$

   On the $\rho = 2$ Bernstein ellipse of each panel mapped to $[-1, 1]$ (vertical semi-axis $\frac{3}{8}H \le \frac{3}{16} < \frac{1}{2}$ in the $t$-plane), L. N. Trefethen (*Is Gauss quadrature better than Clenshaw–Curtis?*, *SIAM Rev.* **50**(1), 67–87, 2008, Theorem 4.1, (4.2), and Theorem 4.3, (4.11), [10.1137/060659831](https://doi.org/10.1137/060659831)) together with Gauss exactness through degree $2p - 1$ and positive weights bounds each panel error before Mellin normalization by $8 H B_m 4^{-p}$ (where $B_m$ bounds the integrand on that panel's ellipse uniformly in $a, b$); multiplying by $\frac{1}{2\pi}$ and summing across all $2M$ panels gives $\lVert A^{(T)}_N - \widehat{A}_N\rVert_2 \le \frac{4N}{\pi} 4^{-p}\sum_{m=-M}^{M-1} H B_m \le C_1 N \cdot 4^{-p}$. Applying the three-lines convexity estimate for the pole-removed function $\Phi(s) := \frac{s-1}{s+1}\zeta(s)$ on the closed substrip $\lvert\operatorname{Im} z\rvert \le \frac{3}{16}$ and integrating in 128-bit Arb (full panel-envelope derivation and script in the [Issue #893 addendum](https://github.com/the-omega-institute/trureturing-books/issues/893#issuecomment-7072893626)) yields the **explicit $T$-uniform constant $C_1 = 5.86\times 10^8$**; hence for any $\Delta_{\mathrm{quad}} \in (0, 1]$, taking $p = \max\bigl(1, \lceil\log_4(C_1 N / \Delta_{\mathrm{quad}})\rceil\bigr) = O(\ln(N/\Delta_{\mathrm{quad}}))$ keeps the quadrature error below $\Delta_{\mathrm{quad}}$.

   For each fixed Gauss index $j$, applying $V^{(j)}$ and $(V^{(j)})^*$ reduces to one 1D Type-1 and one adjoint Type-2 NUFFT. Taking the power-of-two FFT length $K := 2^{\lceil\log_2(4M)\rceil} \in [4M, 8M) = \Theta(M)$ and nearest lattice points $g_a := \frac{2\pi k_a}{K}$ ($\lvert y_a - g_a\rvert \le \frac{2\pi}{K}$), form the order-$J$ Taylor factors $\widetilde{V}^{(j)}_{m,a} := e^{-im g_a}\sum_{\ell=0}^{J-1}(-im/M)^\ell e^{-i\xi_j y_a}\frac{(M(y_a - g_a))^\ell}{\ell!}$ (scalar real-phase remainder at most $\epsilon_{\mathrm{Tay}} := (\pi/2)^J / J!$) together with stored non-negative weights $\bar{\omega}_{m,j} \ge 0$ ($\delta_w := \sum_{m,j}\lvert\omega_{m,j} - \bar{\omega}_{m,j}\rvert$), denote the complex Hermitian surrogate by $S_{\mathbb{C}} := \sum_{j=1}^p (\widetilde{V}^{(j)})^*\operatorname{diag}(\bar{\omega}_{\cdot,j})\widetilde{V}^{(j)}$, and take its real part explicitly to define the **real symmetric, manifestly positive semidefinite** surrogate $S_{\mathbb{R}} := \operatorname{Re} S_{\mathbb{C}} = \sum_{j=1}^p\bigl((\operatorname{Re}\widetilde{V}^{(j)})^\top \operatorname{diag}(\bar{\omega}_{\cdot,j})(\operatorname{Re}\widetilde{V}^{(j)}) + (\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{V}^{(j)})^\top \operatorname{diag}(\bar{\omega}_{\cdot,j})(\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{V}^{(j)})\bigr) \succeq 0$: for any real vector $x \in \mathbb{R}^N$, $S_{\mathbb{R}} x = \operatorname{Re}(S_{\mathbb{C}} x)$ remains strictly real even when grid rounding breaks conjugate pairing, and because $\widehat{A}_N$ is real one has $\lVert \widehat{A}_N - S_{\mathbb{R}}\rVert_2 \le \lVert \widehat{A}_N - S_{\mathbb{C}}\rVert_2$. Whenever the quadrature error is at most $1$, the weight sum obeys $\sum_{m,j}\omega_{m,j} = (\widehat{A}_N)_{11} \le C + 1 =: W_{\mathrm{tot}}$, and summing row bounds gives the explicit operator-norm budget $\lVert \widehat{A}_N - S_{\mathbb{R}}\rVert_2 \le N W_{\mathrm{tot}}(2\epsilon_{\mathrm{Tay}} + \epsilon_{\mathrm{Tay}}^2) + N(1 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{Tay}})^2 \delta_w$: for any $\Delta_{\mathrm{sur}} \in (0, 1]$, choosing $J = 4 + \max\bigl(0, \lceil\log_2(6 N W_{\mathrm{tot}} / \Delta_{\mathrm{sur}})\rceil\bigr) = O(\ln(N/\Delta_{\mathrm{sur}}))$ and $\delta_w \le \frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{sur}}}{8N}$ guarantees $\lVert \widehat{A}_N - S_{\mathbb{R}}\rVert_2 \le \Delta_{\mathrm{sur}}$ at cost $O\bigl(p J(M\ln M + N)\bigr)$ operations and $O(p(M + N))$ storage per matvec (as a separate production-NUFFT check, FINUFFT—A. H. Barnett, J. Magland & L. af Klinteberg, *A Parallel Nonuniform Fast Fourier Transform Library Based on an "Exponential of Semicircle" Kernel*, *SIAM J. Sci. Comput.* **41**(5), C479–C504, 2019, §§3.1.1, 3.2, and 4.1, [10.1137/18M120885X](https://doi.org/10.1137/18M120885X)—was independently cross-checked against dense quadrature matvecs $\widehat{A}_N x$ at $N = 128,\ p = 8,\ T \in \{10, 100, 400\}$, agreeing to relative discrepancies $3.66\times 10^{-14}$, $3.04\times 10^{-13}$, and $2.55\times 10^{-14}$; this checks production NUFFT accuracy separately from the Taylor–FFT certificate budget above).

   Recall from item 2 of [@rem-ch34-nbb-gram-spectral-and-pruning] the unconditional spectral bounds $L := \frac{1}{36 N H_N^2} \le \lambda_{\min}(A_N)$ and $C N \ge \lambda_{\max}(A_N)$. Whenever the real symmetric surrogate $S_{\mathbb{R}}$ satisfies $\lVert A_N - S_{\mathbb{R}}\rVert_2 \le \eta \le \frac{L}{2}$, the operator inequality $S_{\mathbb{R}} \succeq A_N - \eta I \succeq \frac{1}{2}A_N \succ 0$ guarantees strict positive definiteness directly, and Weyl's eigenvalue inequality supplies the explicit spectral enclosure $[L/2,\ U_*]$ ($U_* := \frac{3CN}{2}$) and $\kappa_2(S_{\mathbb{R}}) \le 3\kappa_2(A_N) \le 108 C N^2 H_N^2$. For any iterate $\tilde{x} \in \mathbb{R}^N$ with exact algebraic residual $r := \tilde{b} - S_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{x}$ and recovered coefficients $\tilde{c}_a := \sqrt{a}\,\tilde{x}_a$, writing $E := A_N - S_{\mathbb{R}}$, the exact identity $A_N(\tilde{x} - x_*) = E\tilde{x} - r$ yields two **computable $L^2(0, \infty)$ excess-residual certificates**:

   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   d_{\mathrm{true}}^2(\tilde{c}) - d_N^2 &\;=\; \bigl\lVert A_N^{-1/2}(E\tilde{x} - r)\bigr\rVert_2^2 \;\le\; \frac{\bigl(\eta\lVert\tilde{x}\rVert_2 + \lVert r\rVert_2\bigr)^2}{L}, \\
   d_{\mathrm{true}}(\tilde{c}) - d_N &\;\le\; \sqrt{d_{\mathrm{true}}^2(\tilde{c}) - d_N^2} \;\le\; \frac{\eta\lVert\tilde{x}\rVert_2 + \lVert r\rVert_2}{\sqrt{L}}.
   \end{aligned}
   $$

   Moreover, $S_{\mathbb{R}} \succeq \frac{1}{2}A_N$ and $\tilde{b}^\top A_N^{-1}\tilde{b} = 1 - d_N^2 \le 1$ give the a priori bound $\lVert\tilde{x}\rVert_2 \le \frac{2}{\sqrt{L}} + \frac{2\lVert r\rVert_2}{L}$. For any $0 < \tau \le 1$ (setting $\tau := \min(1, \varepsilon)$ for $d_{\mathrm{true}}(\tilde{c}) - d_N \le \varepsilon$, or $\tau := \min(1, \sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sq}}})$ for $d_{\mathrm{true}}^2(\tilde{c}) - d_N^2 \le \varepsilon_{\mathrm{sq}}$), enforcing $\eta \le \frac{\tau L}{6}$ and $\lVert r\rVert_2 \le R_* := \frac{\tau\sqrt{L}}{6}$ bounds the **squared certificate right-hand side** by $(\frac{5}{9}\tau)^2$ (and the norm certificate right-hand side by $\frac{5}{9}\tau$). Conjugate Gradient (CG) reaches this target in $O(N H_N\ln(N/\tau))$ iterations in exact arithmetic; to avoid floating-point orthogonality loss, **Chebyshev iteration** (Y. Saad, *Iterative Methods for Sparse Linear Systems*, second edition, SIAM, 2003, §12.3.2, Algorithm 12.1, [10.1137/1.9780898718003](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898718003); setting $\rho_{\mathrm{ch}} := \frac{\sqrt{U_*/(L/2)} - 1}{\sqrt{U_*/(L/2)} + 1}$ and taking the least integer $k \ge 1$ with $4 U_*\sqrt{CN}\,\rho_{\mathrm{ch}}^k / L \le R_*/4$, so $k = O(N H_N\ln(N/\tau))$) reduces the exact residual below $R_*/4$, while a local vector error at step $j$ is amplified by at most $k - j + 1$ at step $k$. Fixing the item-3 split budget at $\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}} = \Delta_{\mathrm{quad}} = \Delta_{\mathrm{sur}} := \frac{\tau L}{18}$, in the explicit fixed-point gate schedule (detailed in the [Issue #893 addendum](https://github.com/the-omega-institute/trureturing-books/issues/893#issuecomment-7072893626)), using pairwise sums on scatter collisions and $(j, \ell)$ reductions and counting each unnormalized radix-2 FFT butterfly $v \pm cw$ as one gate yields matvec depth $D_{\mathrm{op}} := 2\log_2 K + \lceil\log_2 N\rceil + \lceil\log_2 J\rceil + \lceil\log_2(Jp)\rceil + 5$ and total step depth $D_{\mathrm{tot}} := D_{\mathrm{op}} + 6$ (including the Chebyshev update); setting iterate envelope $X_{\mathrm{env}} := 1 + \sqrt{CN} + \frac{4\sqrt{CN}}{L}$, growth base $\beta_{\mathrm{gate}} := 2(1 + \max\{2, W_{\mathrm{tot}} + 1\})$, amplification factor $\Gamma_{\mathrm{op}} := \sqrt{N}(1 + 3D_{\mathrm{tot}})\beta_{\mathrm{gate}}^{D_{\mathrm{tot}}}(1 + X_{\mathrm{env}})$, and explicit bit-precision ($B$ fractional bits and $B$ signed integer bits, step $u := 2^{-B}$)

   $$
   B \;:=\; \left\lceil\log_2\max\!\left\{2\beta_{\mathrm{gate}}^{D_{\mathrm{tot}}}(1 + X_{\mathrm{env}}),\;\frac{16 M p N}{\Delta_{\mathrm{sur}}},\;\frac{4\max(1, U_*)\,\Gamma_{\mathrm{op}}\,k(k + 1)}{R_*}\right\}\right\rceil + 2 \;=\; O\!\left(\ln\frac{N T}{\tau}\right),
   $$

   precomputing Gauss abscissas $\xi_j$ and weights $\alpha_{m,j}$ with sufficient guard bits (absorbing the node-shift perturbation of $w(t_{m,j})$ into the $u/4$ weight-arithmetic allowance of item 3) so that all stored weights $\bar{\omega}_{m,j}$ (relative to exact $\omega_{m,j}$ at exact nodes $t_{m,j}$), all Taylor phase and scaling multipliers ($e^{-i\xi_j y_a}\frac{(M(y_a - g_a))^\ell}{\ell!}$ and $(-im/M)^\ell$), FFT twiddles, Chebyshev scalars, and $\tilde{b}$ satisfy absolute error $\le u$ per coordinate and rounding each gate output once to $\le u$ simultaneously ensures: (i) $\delta_w \le 2Mpu \le \frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{sur}}}{8N}$, (ii) per-step error $\le \Gamma_{\mathrm{op}} u$ and $k$-step accumulated iterate error $\le \frac{k(k+1)}{2}\Gamma_{\mathrm{op}} u \le 1$ (inductively preserving the envelope $X_{\mathrm{env}}$ and adding $\le R_*/8$ to the exact residual, so $\lVert r\rVert_2 \le \frac{3}{8}R_*$), and (iii) final residual evaluation $\hat{r} = \operatorname{fl}(\tilde{b} - \operatorname{fl}(S_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{x}))$ error $\delta_r := \Gamma_{\mathrm{op}} u \le R_*/8$, whence $\lVert\hat{r}\rVert_2 + \delta_r \le \frac{5}{8}R_*$. Computing the exact squared norm of the dyadic vector $\hat{r}$ in exact $2B$-fractional-bit dyadic arithmetic and then performing the outward square root and addition at $B$-bit precision keeps the total outward-rounding slack within the remaining $\frac{3}{8}R_*$, so the outward-rounded certified residual $R_{\mathrm{cert}} := \operatorname{ub}(\operatorname{ub}\lVert\hat{r}\rVert_2 + \delta_r)$ certifies $\lVert r\rVert_2 \le R_{\mathrm{cert}} \le R_*$; substituting $R_{\mathrm{cert}}$ together with $\operatorname{ub}\lVert\tilde{x}\rVert_2$ (or the a priori bound $\frac{2}{\sqrt{L}} + \frac{2 R_{\mathrm{cert}}}{L}$) into the residual certificate completes the end-to-end finite-precision certification.

3. **Fixed-tolerance subcubic complexity table and variable-tolerance scaling: why subconvexity $\mu < \frac{1}{4}$ is the threshold breaking $N^3$ in this upper-bound analysis**: Fixing the split budget $\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}} = \Delta_{\mathrm{quad}} = \Delta_{\mathrm{sur}} := \frac{\tau L}{18} \asymp \frac{\tau}{N\ln^2 N}$, putting $\nu := \frac{1}{1 - 2\mu} \ge 1$, and imposing $T \ge \max\bigl(3, (N/\tau)^\nu\bigr)$ for $N \ge 2$ and $0 < \tau \le 1$: carrying the logarithmic (or $+\delta$) factors from $L^{-1} \asymp N\ln^2 N$ and the tail bound through $T$ yields $T = \widetilde{\Theta}((N/\tau)^\nu)$ for convexity, explicit Weyl, and explicit sub-Weyl, and $T = O_\delta((N/\tau)^{\nu + \delta})$ (with $T \ge \max(3, (N/\tau)^\nu)$) for Bourgain and the external RH/Lindelöf track (`riemannZeta_eq_zero_re : RiemannZetaZerosOnLine`, matching the fixed-$N$ $\frac{\ln T}{T}$ tail asymptotic of item 1), with $R = 2Mp = \widetilde{O}(T)$ quadrature nodes. To convert an absolute approximation error $\epsilon_\zeta \le 1$ in $\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+it)$ or Hardy's $Z(t)$ into a weight error, DLMF (25.2.8) ([dlmf.nist.gov/25.2#E8](https://dlmf.nist.gov/25.2#E8)) at cutoff $1$ gives $\lvert\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+it)\rvert = \lvert Z(t)\rvert \le 4 + 2T =: Z_{\max}$ on $\lvert t\rvert \le T$, so with $\alpha_{m,j} \le H \le \frac{1}{2}$ the weight changes by at most $\gamma_{\mathrm{wt}}\epsilon_\zeta$ ($\gamma_{\mathrm{wt}} := \frac{2}{\pi}(2Z_{\max} + 1)$); using evaluation accuracy $\epsilon_\zeta \le \frac{u}{4\max(1, \gamma_{\mathrm{wt}})}$, allocating $u/4$ to the remaining weight and guard-bit node arithmetic, rounding to the nearest multiple of $u$, and clipping below at $0$ enforces $\lvert\bar{\omega}_{m,j} - \omega_{m,j}\rvert \le u$ and $\delta_w \le 2Mpu$ (so the absolute evaluation-error threshold $\epsilon_\zeta$ is met by a fixed global inverse power $(NT/\tau)^{-C_0}$, corresponding to bit precision $B_\zeta = O(\ln(NT/\tau))$, where $C_0$ is determined by item 2 independently of $N, \tau, T$). Precomputing the weights follows two routes:
   - *Per-point Riemann–Siegel route (with low-height Euler–Maclaurin fallback)*: Above the height threshold in J. Arias de Reyna (*High precision computation of Riemann's zeta function by the Riemann-Siegel formula, I*, *Math. Comp.* **80**(274), 995–1009, 2011, §4, Theorems 4.1–4.2 and (4.7)–(4.8), [10.1090/S0025-5718-2010-02426-3](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0025-5718-2010-02426-3); and *High precision computation of Riemann's zeta function by the Riemann-Siegel formula, II*, [arXiv:2201.00342v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.00342v1), §3.2, Theorem 8, (19)–(20) for prescribed-error truncation), apply Riemann–Siegel evaluation; below that threshold (including near $t = 0$, as in the [Issue #893 main log](https://github.com/the-omega-institute/trureturing-books/issues/893#issuecomment-7072121051)), fall back to the uniform Euler–Maclaurin rule below (costing at most $\widetilde{O}(T)$ total, absorbed into $\widetilde{O}(T^{3/2})$). Evaluating all $R$ weights to $B_\zeta$ bits therefore takes $\widetilde{O}(T^{3/2})$, giving total arithmetic cost $\widetilde{O}\bigl(T^{3/2} + N(T + N)\bigr)$: namely $\widetilde{O}\bigl((N/\tau)^{3\nu/2} + N(N/\tau)^\nu\bigr)$ for convexity and explicit Weyl/sub-Weyl, and $O_\delta\bigl((N/\tau)^{3\nu/2 + \delta} + N(N/\tau)^{\nu + \delta}\bigr)$ for Bourgain and the external RH/Lindelöf track invoking `riemannZeta_eq_zero_re : RiemannZetaZerosOnLine`. At fixed $\tau$, $\frac{3\nu}{2} < 1 + \nu$ for every $\mu \in [0, \frac{1}{4})$, so the iteration term $NT$ dominates; under variable tolerance, comparing the bare polynomial powers shows—for instance in the explicit sub-Weyl track—that the per-point precomputation power $(N/\tau)^{123/55}$ exceeds the iteration power $N^{137/55}\tau^{-82/55}$ once $\tau$ falls below the bare-power crossover $N^{-14/41}$ (up to polylogarithmic factors).
   - *Hybrid low-height / batched Odlyzko–Schönhage route*: For low-height nodes $\lvert t\rvert \le T^{1/2}$ (including $t = 0$), apply Euler–Maclaurin summation (DLMF §25.2(iii), eq. (25.2.9), [dlmf.nist.gov/25.2#E9](https://dlmf.nist.gov/25.2#E9); F. Johansson, *Rigorous high-precision computation of the Hurwitz zeta function and its derivatives*, *Numer. Algor.* **69**, 253–270, 2015, §2, eqs. (5)–(9), and Theorem 1, eqs. (12)–(14), [10.1007/s11075-014-9893-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11075-014-9893-1); see also G. A. Hiary, *Fast methods to compute the Riemann zeta function*, *Ann. of Math.* (2) **174**(2), 891–946, 2011, §1 immediately after Theorem 1.1, [10.4007/annals.2011.174.2.4](https://doi.org/10.4007/annals.2011.174.2.4), preprint [arXiv:0711.5005v4](https://arxiv.org/abs/0711.5005v4)): by the Fourier-mode remainder estimate in the [Issue #893 addendum](https://github.com/the-omega-institute/trureturing-books/issues/893#issuecomment-7072893626), choosing $N_{\mathrm{EM}} = \lceil 2(\lvert t\rvert + B_\zeta)\rceil$ and $K_{\mathrm{EM}} = \lceil B_\zeta\rceil$ bounds the remainder by $< 2^{-B_\zeta - 1}$ uniformly for all real $t$ (down to $t = 0$), using $\Theta(1 + \lvert t\rvert + B_\zeta)$ terms per node and $\widetilde{O}(T)$ total work across all $\widetilde{O}(T^{1/2})$ low-height nodes. On $T^{1/2} \le \lvert t\rvert \le T$, cover by windows of length $X^{1/2}$ at height $X$ via A. M. Odlyzko & A. Schönhage (*Fast algorithms for multiple evaluations of the Riemann zeta function*, *Trans. Amer. Math. Soc.* **309**(2), 797–809, 1988, [10.1090/S0002-9947-1988-0961614-2](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0002-9947-1988-0961614-2); critical-line Theorem 5.1 as restated in G. A. Hiary, *An amortized-complexity method to compute the Riemann zeta function*, *Math. Comp.* **80**(275), 1785–1796, 2011, §1, Algorithm 1.2, [10.1090/S0025-5718-2011-02452-X](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0025-5718-2011-02452-X), preprint [arXiv:1002.2443v3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1002.2443v3)): since $T \ge (N/\tau)^\nu \ge N/\tau$ and every high-segment window has $X \ge T^{1/2}$, we have $N T / \tau \le T^2 \le X^4$, so local accuracy $X^{-4 C_0}$ **suffices** for global accuracy $(NT/\tau)^{-C_0}$. Absorbing the logarithmic factors from $R = \widetilde{O}(T)$ and the low-height $\widetilde{O}(T)$ pass into part of the arbitrarily small $+\delta$ allowance yields batched precomputation cost $O_\delta(T^{1+\delta})$ across all $0 < \tau \le 1$, so both precomputation and iteration $\widetilde{O}(NT)$ sit within the combined total upper bound $O_\delta(N(N/\tau)^{\nu+\delta})$.

   At fixed tolerance $\tau$, the total time exponent $1 + \frac{1}{1 - 2\mu} < 3$ in this upper-bound analysis holds if and only if $\mu < \frac{1}{4}$: **elementary convexity ($\mu = \frac{1}{4}$) sits directly on the cubic $N^3$ barrier of this framework, and every subconvexity exponent gives an unconditionally subcubic upper bound**. Convexity, explicit Weyl, explicit sub-Weyl, and explicit RH invoking [@conj-riemann-hypothesis] (`riemannZeta_eq_zero_re : RiemannZetaZerosOnLine`) via Simonič (2022) plus finite-interval Arb verification each yield a fully explicit computable cutoff $T$ for concrete $N$, whereas Bourgain (2017) Theorem 5 carries an unspecified implicit constant (without a numerical value) and therefore supplies an unconditional asymptotic complexity exponent $71/29$ (up to $+\delta$) rather than a finite-$N$ explicit numerical cutoff unless paired with a numerical constant or certified via an explicit tail bound. Fixed-tolerance polynomial exponents in $N$ are summarized below (for variable tolerance $\tau$, substitute $T = \widetilde{\Theta}((N/\tau)^\nu)$ or $O_\delta((N/\tau)^{\nu+\delta})$ carrying the logarithmic or $+\delta$ factors while imposing $T \ge \max\bigl(3, (N/\tau)^\nu\bigr)$, matching the fixed-$N$ $\frac{\ln T}{T}$ tail asymptotic of item 1; polylogarithmic factors appear in the non-batched columns for convexity, explicit Weyl, and explicit sub-Weyl, while arbitrarily small $+\delta$ losses appear in the Bourgain, Lindelöf, and batched columns):

   | Arithmetic input for $\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+it)$ and source | Cutoff $T$ & storage exponent | Per-point Riemann–Siegel precomp. exponent | Batched precomp. exponent | Iteration & total time exponent |
   | :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
   | Elementary convexity ($\mu = \frac{1}{4}$; e.g. $84e(1+\lvert t\rvert)^{1/4}\ln(2+\lvert t\rvert)$) | $2$ | $3$ | $2$ | $3$ (cubic barrier) |
   | **Explicit unconditional Weyl** ($\mu = \frac{1}{6}$; Hiary–Patel–Yang 2024 Thm. 1.1, $0.618\,t^{1/6}\ln t$, $t \ge 3$) | $3/2$ | $9/4$ | $3/2$ | **$5/2$** (fully explicit subcubic) |
   | **Explicit unconditional sub-Weyl** ($\mu = \frac{27}{164}$; Patel–Yang 2024 Thm. 1.1, $66.7\,t^{27/164}$, $t \ge 3$) | $82/55$ | $123/55$ | $82/55$ | **$137/55 \approx 2.491$** (fully explicit subcubic) |
   | **Unconditional Bourgain** ($\mu = \frac{13}{84}$, arbitrarily small $+\delta$ loss; Bourgain 2017 Thm. 5, unspecified implicit constant) | $42/29$ | $63/29$ | $42/29$ | **$71/29 \approx 2.448$** (unconditional asymptotic subcubic) |
   | **External RH via Lindelöf** ($\mu = 0$; `riemannZeta_eq_zero_re : RiemannZetaZerosOnLine` $+$ Littlewood 1924 / Simonič 2022) | **$1$** (near-linear) | $3/2$ | **$1$** | **$2$** (quasi-quadratic) |

   Exact references for the unconditional rows: G. A. Hiary, D. Patel & A. Yang, *An improved explicit estimate for $\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+it)$*, *J. Number Theory* **256**, 195–217, 2024, Theorem 1.1, [10.1016/j.jnt.2023.09.003](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jnt.2023.09.003) (independently verified on $3 \le t \le 200$ here via 80-bit Arb subdivision over $2{,}047$ intervals); D. Patel & A. Yang, *An explicit sub-Weyl bound for $\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+it)$*, *J. Number Theory* **262**, 301–334, 2024, Theorem 1.1, [10.1016/j.jnt.2024.03.011](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jnt.2024.03.011); J. Bourgain, *Decoupling, exponential sums and the Riemann zeta function*, *J. Amer. Math. Soc.* **30**(1), 205–224, 2017, Theorem 5, [10.1090/jams/860](https://doi.org/10.1090/jams/860). The RH row explicitly invokes `riemannZeta_eq_zero_re : RiemannZetaZerosOnLine` from [@conj-riemann-hypothesis] together with J. E. Littlewood (*On the zeros of the Riemann zeta-function*, *Proc. Cambridge Philos. Soc.* **22**(3), 295–318, 1924, §23, Theorem 12, p. 317, [10.1017/S0305004100014225](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0305004100014225)) and A. Simonič (*Explicit Estimates for $\zeta(s)$ in the Critical Strip Under the Riemann Hypothesis*, *Q. J. Math.* **73**(3), 1055–1087, 2022, Theorem 1, eq. (8), and §3, Corollary 2, [10.1093/qmath/haac007](https://doi.org/10.1093/qmath/haac007)).

4. **High-precision Arb/FLINT benchmarks: sharp conditioning transition near the Nyquist scale ($T \approx \pi N$), head-to-head contrast against per-pivot pruning, and off-diagonal rank-plateau comparison (`NUM`)**: Fixing $H = \frac{1}{8}$ and $p = 8$ (against a certified $p = 26$ continuous-integral reference) across all $24$ pairs $(N, T) \in \{16, 32, 64, 128\} \times \{10, 25, 50, 100, 200, 400\}$ in Arb (up to $4{,}200$-bit precision on the most ill-conditioned cases), we construct $\widehat{A}_N$ and compare against the exact Vasyunin matrix $A_N$: all $24$ parameter sets have strictly positive quadrature weights and **strictly positive definite surrogates (zero negative eigenvalues)**, while for $N = 16, 32, 64, 128$ respectively the quadrature error $\lVert A^{(T)}_N - \widehat{A}_N\rVert_2$ holds across all six cutoffs $T$ at $1.03\times 10^{-18}$, $2.15\times 10^{-18}$, $4.46\times 10^{-18}$, and $9.23\times 10^{-18}$, so the total spectral error matches $\lVert E^{(T)}_N\rVert_2$ to shown precision (reference $d_N^2$ rounded to six decimal places: $0.017894, 0.014052, 0.011376, 0.009659$). Key transition rows are listed below (both excess columns and the squared certificate apply to the exact quadrature linear solve $\widehat{A}_N x = \tilde{b}$ with exact algebraic residual $r = 0$; excesses are rounded to four significant digits, and squared certificates are recomputed in Arb using the measured spectral-error upper endpoint and a certified lower endpoint for $\lambda_{\min}(A_N)$ and **rounded outward upward** to four significant digits so every printed entry is a valid upper bound; separate float64 CG and Chebyshev runs use dense matvecs stopping when the iterate's recursive residual falls below $10^{-10}$, capped at $100{,}000$ steps—on extreme ill-conditioned sub-Nyquist rows such as $N = 128,\ T = 200$ where $\lambda_{\min} \approx 8.4\times 10^{-35}$, the float64 recomputed residual $\lVert\tilde{b} - \widehat{A}_N x\rVert_2 \approx 2.18\times 10^{-10}$ can slightly exceed the recursive stopping value—with Chebyshev shown for the unconditional $L/2$ enclosure / measured enclosure; "—" denotes a skipped run and "†" denotes hitting the $100{,}000$-step cap or float64 loss of positive curvature):

   | $N$ | $T$ | $\lVert E^{(T)}_N\rVert_2$ | $\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{A}_N)$ | Squared excess $d_{\mathrm{true}}^2 - d_N^2$ | Norm excess $d_{\mathrm{true}} - d_N$ | Squared certificate (outward) | CG steps | Chebyshev steps (uncond. / measured) |
   | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
   | $16$ | $25$ | $0.15009$ | $1.400\times 10^{-5}$ | $8.167\times 10^{-1}$ | $7.798\times 10^{-1}$ | $7.531$ | $23$ | — / $13{,}360$ |
   | $16$ | $50$ | $0.08888$ | $3.405\times 10^{-2}$ | $5.130\times 10^{-4}$ | $1.904\times 10^{-3}$ | $0.3458$ | $19$ | $6{,}958$ / $273$ |
   | $32$ | $50$ | $0.10692$ | $7.195\times 10^{-10}$ | $1.523\times 10^{2}$ | $1.222\times 10^{1}$ | $1.910\times 10^{3}$ | $114$ | — / † |
   | $32$ | $100$ | $0.07073$ | $1.710\times 10^{-2}$ | $1.553\times 10^{-4}$ | $6.533\times 10^{-4}$ | $0.4612$ | $44$ | $17{,}060$ / $547$ |
   | $64$ | $100$ | $0.08536$ | $5.078\times 10^{-18}$ | $6.252\times 10^{10}$ | $2.500\times 10^{5}$ | $1.566\times 10^{12}$ | † | — / — |
   | $64$ | $200$ | $0.04753$ | $9.661\times 10^{-3}$ | $4.100\times 10^{-5}$ | $1.920\times 10^{-4}$ | $0.4023$ | $75$ | $39{,}603$ / $1{,}035$ |
   | $128$ | $200$ | $0.05518$ | $8.421\times 10^{-35}$ | $1.956\times 10^{26}$ | $1.399\times 10^{13}$ | $6.650\times 10^{27}$ | $4{,}567$ | — / — |
   | $128$ | $400$ | $0.03400$ | $4.814\times 10^{-3}$ | $1.511\times 10^{-5}$ | $7.682\times 10^{-5}$ | $0.4077$ | $136$ | $91{,}993$ / $2{,}082$ |

   Three structural lessons stand out:
   - **Sharp conditioning transition near the Nyquist scale ($T \approx \pi N$)**: Because the upper frequencies $\ln a$ cluster at spacing $\sim 1/N$, at cutoffs well below $\pi N$ (such as $T = 10, 100, 200$ for $N = 128$, where $T/(\pi N) < 0.498$), $\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{A}_N)$ drops sharply to $2.406\times 10^{-357}$, $2.681\times 10^{-101}$, and $8.421\times 10^{-35}$; once $T$ reaches $T \approx 0.995\,\pi N$ ($T = 50, 100, 200, 400$ for $N = 16, 32, 64, 128$, all with $T/(\pi N) \approx 0.9947$), $\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{A}_N)$ rebounds to the $10^{-3}\text{–}10^{-2}$ scale (comparable to $1/N$), squared excesses fall to order $10^{-5}\text{–}10^{-4}$ (from $5.130\times 10^{-4}$ down to $1.511\times 10^{-5}$), and float64 CG converges in $19$–$136$ iterations.
   - **Like-for-like contrast against per-pivot Schur pruning in [@rem-ch34-nbb-gram-spectral-and-pruning] ($N = 128$)**: In the original $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_{128}$ coordinates, per-pivot Schur pruning at $\tau = 10^{-4}$ incurs $0.060001\%$ relative error yet produces **$14$ negative eigenvalues** and blows the total true squared residual up to $3.539017$ (a **squared excess** $d_{\mathrm{true}}^2 - d_{128}^2 \approx 3.529359$, with total squared residual $366\times$ optimal $d_{128}^2 \approx 0.009659$); at comparable original relative error **$0.053419\%$** ($T = 1600$), the spectral surrogate stays **strictly positive definite** ($\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{A}_{128}) \approx 0.006411$) with **squared excess only $7.112\times 10^{-7}$** (total squared residual $\approx 0.009659$, norm excess $3.618\times 10^{-6}$, outward-rounded measured-$\lambda_{\min}$ squared certificate $0.04604$, $122$ CG steps; at $T = 2000$, relative error $0.044300\%$, squared excess $4.607\times 10^{-7}$, outward squared certificate $0.03275$). Comparing strictly like for like, the spectral solver reduces **squared excess by a factor of $\approx 4.96\times 10^6$ (over six orders of magnitude)** and **total squared residual by a factor of $\approx 366$**.
   - **Taylor rank bound for fixed-cutoff off-diagonal blocks and rank-plateau comparison**: On any off-diagonal block $I \times J$ (whose narrower logarithmic interval has half-width $W$), truncated Taylor expansion of the kernel phase $e^{it\ln(b/a)}$ about the narrow-side logarithmic midpoint (full derivation in the Issue #893 main log) bounds the numerical rank of the truncated block $A^{(T)}_N\big|_{I\times J}$ at tolerance $\tau_{\mathrm{blk}} \in (0, 1)$ relative to the natural block scale $C\sqrt{\lvert I\rvert\lvert J\rvert}$ by $O\bigl(T W + \ln(1/\tau_{\mathrm{blk}}) + 1\bigr)$ (if normalized by the measured block norm instead, that norm enters the logarithm). Certified numerical ranks at each block's own $10^{-4}$ relative threshold (separated block / adjacent half-block across $N = 64 \to 128 \to 256$) grow for exact $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ (`12 / 24` $\to$ `19 / 39` $\to$ `23 / 42`; scaled $A_N$: `11 / 24` $\to$ `19 / 38` $\to$ `24 / 48`), whereas truncated $A^{(T)}_N$ plateaus at $T = 10$ (`4 / 5`), at $T = 25$ (`4 / 8`), at $T = 50$ (`6 / 12` $\to$ `7 / 13` $\to$ `7 / 13`), and at $T = 100$ (`9 / 20` $\to$ `11 / 24` $\to$ `11 / 24`). These fixed-$T$ plateaus are consistent with the Taylor rank bound and suggest that high-frequency modes $\lvert t\rvert > T$ may underlie the full-block rank climb observed in [@rem-ch34-nbb-gram-spectral-and-pruning]; however, because each matrix is normalized by its own block norm (and $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ vs. $A_N$ also differ by diagonal scaling), these data alone do not establish that the rank growth requires the high-frequency tail, and the rigorous common-absolute-threshold attribution and analytic lower bound follow immediately in [@rem-ch34-nbb-preconditioning-and-hmatrix-obstruction].
:::

::: {.remark #rem-ch34-nbb-preconditioning-and-hmatrix-obstruction title="Genuine $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$: Quasilinear $O(N)$ Tridiagonal Laplace Preconditioning, $O(N\ln N)$ Spatial-Truncation Preconditioning, and Separated-Block $\mathcal{H}$-Matrix Compression Obstruction (Written Derivation, Literature, and 256/320-Bit Arb Verification)"}
**Provenance and verification status.** This remark directly resolves the first core target of [@prob-ch34-arithmetic-schur-subcubic-question], building on the notation and frozen anchors of [@rem-ch34-nbb-gram-spectral-and-pruning] and [@rem-ch34-nbb-subcubic-spectral-solver] ([`fractionalMellin_eq_zeta`](lean:D5/S3/Weil/ZetaPntBounds/NymanFractionalMellin.fractionalMellin_eq_zeta), [`fractional_reciprocal_inner_product`](lean:D5/S3/Constants/InnerProducts/FractionalReciprocalInnerProduct.fractional_reciprocal_inner_product), [`nyman_beurling_finite_gram_distance`](lean:D5/S3/Observer/Hilbert/NymanBeurlingFiniteGramDistance.nyman_beurling_finite_gram_distance), and [`riemann_zeta_dirichlet_sum_eq_euler_product`](lean:D5/S3/Zeros/ZetaUpgrade.riemann_zeta_dirichlet_sum_eq_euler_product) for the unconditional Euler-product lower bound in item 1) to establish simultaneously, for genuine $G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N$ and $A_N = D^{1/2} G^{\mathrm{NB}}_N D^{1/2}$, a **spatial-side separated-block $\mathcal{H}$-matrix compression no-go theorem** and a **fast-preconditioned quasilinear solver breakthrough**: the unconditional tracks of items 1–2 and all core derivations of items 3–4 are independent of [@conj-riemann-hypothesis], whereas the RH/Lindelöf tracks of items 1, 2, and 4 explicitly invoke the externally proved terminal declaration `riemannZeta_eq_zero_re : RiemannZetaZerosOnLine` recorded in [@conj-riemann-hypothesis]. The written derivations and dual-precision ($256$-bit / $320$-bit, `python-flint 0.9.0`) FLINT/Arb interval certifications below are not formalized in the pinned `trureturing` repository (`manual derivation` / `NUM`; all $13{,}013$ serialized Arb balls achieve $100\%$ overlap and all $1{,}293$ integer counts achieve $100\%$ agreement; verification summary, five-file pinned public Gist revision containing `verify_cycle11.py`, `crosscheck_cycle11.py`, `SHA256SUMS`, `crosscheck.log`, and `summary_tables.md`, and complete eight-file SHA-256 manifest archived at [Issue #1016 verification summary](https://github.com/the-omega-institute/trureturing-books/issues/1016#issuecomment-7085618837), [manifest and checksum archive](https://github.com/the-omega-institute/trureturing-books/issues/1016#issuecomment-7085619502), and [pinned Gist revision](https://gist.github.com/loning/25a4a189dd02b0dd466e4cab57c65cd8/80ca873d12b1f9236e79d5b436d2a8f4556d7687); core file checksums are `verify_cycle11.py` `58a18feec00dde73ecea4b0c4a75b7df5f30642c972ce2044d390e209c6c3a90`, `crosscheck_cycle11.py` `2947cb0b3869126201b0c04f5f29cf134dae65bf4ad8f692636c7595e039b4e3`, `run_256.log` `51ccc6b4b8230696cf9f43dbcbb85d775d674f1c361811c1f3bce086d9a52ef8`, and `run_320.log` `f7ce7adc18d9aa3188e59f1f78de49e5c8a3da21452d52c3d761380af5b43186`). Four core conclusions follow:

1. **Dual-track spectral theory of the exact $O(N)$ tridiagonal Laplace preconditioner $K_N = K^{\mathrm{Lap}}(\frac{1}{2})$ (unconditional Euler-product floor and $N^{o(1)}$ condition-number collapse under external RH)**: Define the log-Laplace kernel matrix $(K_N)_{ab} := \sqrt{\frac{\min(a,b)}{\max(a,b)}} = \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{e^{it\ln(b/a)}}{\frac{1}{4}+t^2}\,dt$ and its unscaled counterpart $\widetilde{K}_N := D^{-1/2} K_N D^{-1/2} = \bigl[\frac{1}{\max(a,b)}\bigr]_{a,b=1}^N$. For any $c \in \mathbb{C}^N$, setting $C_m := \sum_{a=1}^m c_a$ and $x_a := c_a / \sqrt{a}$ yields by summation over integer intervals $x^* K_N x = c^* \widetilde{K}_N c = \sum_{m=1}^{N-1}\frac{\lvert C_m\rvert^2}{m(m+1)} + \frac{\lvert C_N\rvert^2}{N} = \int_0^1 \lvert\sum_{a < 1/y} c_a\rvert^2\,dy$. Letting $B_N$ be the first-difference bidiagonal matrix ($(B_N)_{ii} = 1$, $(B_N)_{i+1,i} = -1$, so $B_N^{-1}$ is the prefix-sum matrix) and $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Lap}} := \operatorname{diag}\bigl(\frac{1}{1\cdot 2}, \dots, \frac{1}{(N-1)N}, \frac{1}{N}\bigr)$, we obtain the exact factorization $\widetilde{K}_N = (B_N^{-1})^\top \Lambda_{\mathrm{Lap}} B_N^{-1}$ and **explicit tridiagonal inverse**

   $$
   \widetilde{K}_N^{-1} \;=\; B_N \Lambda_{\mathrm{Lap}}^{-1} B_N^\top, \qquad (K_N^{-1})_{a,a} \;=\; \begin{cases} 2a, & 1 \le a \le N - 1, \\ N, & a = N, \end{cases} \qquad (K_N^{-1})_{a,a+1} \;=\; (K_N^{-1})_{a+1,a} \;=\; -\sqrt{a(a + 1)},
   $$

   so applying $K_N^{-1}$ takes **$O(N)$ arithmetic operations and $O(N)$ storage** (zero factorization); Gershgorin's circle theorem gives $2a + \sqrt{a(a-1)} + \sqrt{a(a+1)} < 4a < 4N$ on every interior row (and $N + \sqrt{N(N-1)} < 2N$ on the last row), hence unconditionally for all $N \ge 1$, $\lVert K_N^{-1}\rVert_2 < 4N$ (yielding $\lVert x\rVert_2^2 \le 4N\,(x^* K_N x)$). Four sharp spectral bounds follow for $K_N^{-1/2} A_N K_N^{-1/2}$:
   - *Unconditional and RH/Lindelöf upper spectral bounds*: Put $D(t) := \sum_{a=1}^N x_a a^{-it}$ and $M_\zeta(T) := \max_{\lvert t\rvert \le T}\lvert\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+it)\rvert$. Splitting $x^* A_N x = \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^\infty \frac{\lvert\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+it)\rvert^2}{\frac{1}{4}+t^2}\lvert D(t)\rvert^2\,dt$ at any $T \ge 1$ into $\lvert t\rvert \le T$ and $\lvert t\rvert > T$ bounds the head by $M_\zeta(T)^2 (x^* K_N x)$ and the tail by $\lVert E^{(T)}_N\rVert_2\lVert x\rVert_2^2 \le 4N\lVert E^{(T)}_N\rVert_2 (x^* K_N x)$, giving $\lambda_{\max}(K_N^{-1/2} A_N K_N^{-1/2}) \le M_\zeta(T)^2 + 4N\lVert E^{(T)}_N\rVert_2$. Setting $T = N$ and inserting the Montgomery–Vaughan tail bound of [@rem-ch34-nbb-subcubic-spectral-solver] item 1, whenever $\lvert\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+it)\rvert \ll_\delta (1 + \lvert t\rvert)^{\mu + \delta}$ ($\mu \in [0, \frac{1}{4}]$), we obtain $\lambda_{\max}(K_N^{-1/2} A_N K_N^{-1/2}) \ll_\delta N^{2\mu + \delta}$ (explicit convexity $\widetilde{O}(N^{1/2})$, explicit Weyl $\widetilde{O}(N^{1/3})$, explicit sub-Weyl $O(N^{27/82})$, Bourgain $O_\delta(N^{13/42 + \delta})$, and under external [@conj-riemann-hypothesis] via Littlewood 1924 / Simonič 2022, $\lambda_{\max}(K_N^{-1/2} A_N K_N^{-1/2}) \le \exp\bigl(O(\frac{\ln N}{\ln\ln N})\bigr) = N^{o(1)}$). Conversely, the adjacent-difference test vector $x_{\mathrm{diff}} := \sqrt{N}\,e_N - \sqrt{N-1}\,e_{N-1}$ satisfies the exact identities $x_{\mathrm{diff}}^\top K_N x_{\mathrm{diff}} = \frac{(N-1)^2}{(N-1)N} + \frac{1}{N} \equiv 1$ and $x_{\mathrm{diff}}^\top A_N x_{\mathrm{diff}} = L_N$, unconditionally forcing for all $N \ge 2$ that $\lambda_{\max}(K_N^{-1/2} A_N K_N^{-1/2}) \ge L_N = \ln N + 1 - C + O(N^{-1})$.
   - *Right-shifted half-plane and Hardy $H^2$ Blaschke-monotonicity lower bounds (unconditional Euler-product bound and $N^{-o(1)}$ bound under RH)*: Let $P(s) := \sum_{a=1}^N c_a a^{-s}$. For any $\sigma = \frac{1}{2} + \eta > \frac{1}{2}$ ($\eta > 0$), because the inverse Mellin transform of $\frac{a^{-s}}{s}$ on $\operatorname{Re} s = \sigma$ is $\mathbf{1}_{(0, 1/a)}(y)$, Plancherel's identity gives $\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^\infty \bigl\lvert\frac{P(\sigma + it)}{\sigma + it}\bigr\rvert^2 dt = \int_0^1 \lvert\sum_{a < 1/y} c_a\rvert^2 y^{2\eta}\,dy$. Splitting $[0, 1]$ at $y = \frac{1}{N}$, on $[\frac{1}{N}, 1]$ we have $y^{2\eta} \ge N^{-2\eta}$, whereas on $(0, \frac{1}{N})$ the squared prefix sum $\lvert\sum_{a < 1/y} c_a\rvert^2 \equiv \lvert C_N\rvert^2$ is constant with exact ratio $\frac{\int_0^{1/N} dy}{\int_0^{1/N} y^{2\eta}\,dy} = (1 + 2\eta) N^{2\eta}$, yielding for all $N \ge 1$, $\eta > 0$, and $x \in \mathbb{C}^N$ the unconditional line-shift inequality $x^* K_N x \le (1 + 2\eta)\,N^{2\eta}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigl\lvert\frac{P(\frac{1}{2} + \eta + it)}{\frac{1}{2} + \eta + it}\bigr\rvert^2 dt$.
